{"step_index":2,"source":"MODEL","type":"GENERIC","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T08:56:55Z","content":"Created At: 2026-10-10T11:56:55+03:00\nCompleted At: 2026-10-10T11:56:55+03:00\nFile Path: `file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/PLAN.md`\nTotal Lines: 422\nTotal Bytes: 25112\nShowing lines 1 to 422\nThe following code has been modified to include a line number before every line, in the format: <line_number>: <original_line>. Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.\n1: # Ukraine Air Raid Alerts: Real-Time Duration & Expiration Predictor\n2: \n3: > Implementation plan for a pet project (Python only). Phase 1 of a larger time series analysis of air raid alerts in Ukraine.\n4: \n5: ---\n6: \n7: ## 1. Goal and Scope\n8: \n9: Build a small web app that:\n10: \n11: 1. Shows a **map of Ukraine** with oblasts colored by current alert status (active / inactive).\n12: 2. For any region the user selects, shows:\n13:    - **If an alert is active:** predicted end time (with an uncertainty range) and the probability the alert is over within the next 15 / 30 / 60 minutes.\n14:    - **If no alert is active:** predicted time until the next alert starts (with an uncertainty range) and the probability of an alert starting within the next 1 / 3 / 6 hours.\n15: 3. Looks good: light.dark theme, smooth map, clean charts, website-friendly.\n16: \n17: **Out of scope for Phase 1:** raion/hromada-level granularity, attack-type classification (drones vs. missiles), real-time push notifications, any claim of safety-critical accuracy.\n18: \n19: > **Important disclaimer (put it in the UI too):** this is a statistical experiment, not a safety tool. Predictions must never replace official alerts.\n20: \n21: ---\n22: \n23: ## 2. Problem Framing\n24: \n25: The two prediction tasks are really **time-to-event problems**, not classic forecasting:\n26: \n27: | Task | Event | Clock starts | Censoring |\n28: |---|---|---|---|\n29: | **A. Alert duration** | Alert ends | Alert start | Currently active alert is *right-censored* (we know it lasted at least X minutes) |\n30: | **B. Gap to next alert** | Next alert sta\n<truncated 23539 bytes>\n: ## 11. Risks and Mitigations\n391: \n392: | Risk | Mitigation |\n393: |---|---|\n394: | **Low predictability.** Alerts depend on events that aren't in the data | Be honest in the UI; show wide intervals; always compare to baselines |\n395: | **Non-stationarity** (patterns change over time) | Time-based validation, recency weighting, periodic retraining |\n396: | **API access/rate limits/terms changes** | Cache aggressively, keep own history, abstract the data source behind an interface |\n397: | **Data leakage** | Point-in-time features, explicit `as_of_ts`, unit tests |\n398: | **Misuse of predictions as safety guidance** | Prominent disclaimer, no push notifications, no \"safe to leave shelter\" wording |\n399: | **Region/geometry mismatches** | Single `regions.csv` as source of truth, tests for ID coverage |\n400: | **Ethics / sensitivity** | Calm tone, no sensationalism, credit data sources |\n401: \n402: ---\n403: \n404: ## 12. Definition of Done for the MVP\n405: \n406: - Map renders all regions with correct live status, refreshed automatically\n407: - Selecting a region shows either predicted end (active) or predicted next start (inactive) with uncertainty ranges\n408: - Models beat the simple baselines on a time-based backtest, with results documented\n409: - Predictions use only point-in-time features (tested)\n410: - Data staleness and API failures are handled gracefully\n411: - App deployed publicly or runnable with a single command\n412: - README contains screenshots, methodology, metrics, limitations, and a disclaimer\n413: \n414: ---\n415: \n416: ## 13. Key Decisions to Make Early\n417: \n418: 1. Include Crimea / temporarily occupied regions in the model, or only on the map?\n419: 2. Granularity of the time grid for the hazard model (1 min vs. 5 min)\n420: 3. Streamlit + Plotly vs. PyDeck for the map (try Plotly first)\n421: 4. How to host the poller (local machine, GitHub Actions cron, small VPS)\n422: 5. UI language(s): Ukrainian, English, or both\nThe above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.\n","truncated_fields":["content"]}

{"step_index":10,"source":"MODEL","type":"GENERIC","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T08:57:08Z","content":"Created At: 2026-10-10T11:57:08+03:00\nCompleted At: 2026-10-10T11:57:08+03:00\nFile Path: `file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/docs/theory.md`\nTotal Lines: 154\nTotal Bytes: 9869\nShowing lines 1 to 100\nThe following code has been modified to include a line number before every line, in the format: <line_number>: <original_line>. Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.\n1: # Comprehensive Guide to Time Series Analysis\n2: \n3: Time series analysis models data points collected sequentially over time $t \\in T$. Unlike standard statistical modeling, time series data violates the Independent and Identically Distributed (i.i.d.) assumption due to serial dependencies across observations.\n4: \n5: Data structures fall into two main representations:\n6: * **Regular Time Series**: Sampled at uniform intervals $\\Delta t$ (e.g., hourly aggregation, daily sum).\n7: * **Irregular / Point Process Data**: Events occur at arbitrary timestamps $t_1, t_2, \\dots, t_n$. Analysis focuses on inter-arrival times $\\tau_i = t_i - t_{i-1}$, event counts in time windows, or continuous intensity estimation.\n8: \n9: ---\n10: \n11: ## 1. Time Series Decomposition\n12: \n13: A time series $y_t$ can be factorized into four primary component processes:\n14: \n15: 1. **Additive Model**: Used when seasonal/cyclical variations remain constant regardless of the trend magnitude.\n16:    $$y_t = T_t + S_t + C_t + \\epsilon_t$$\n17: 2. **Multiplicative Model**: Used when the amplitude of variations scales proportionally with the trend.\n18:    $$y_t = T_t \\cdot S_t \\cdot C_t \\cdot \\epsilon_t$$\n19: \n20: ### Components\n21: * **Trend ($T_t$)**: Long-term directional movement (upward, downward, or neutral) over the entire timeframe.\n22: * **Seasonality ($S_t$)**: Patterns repeating at fixed, deterministic calendar frequencies (e.g., daily hour-of-day, weekly day-of-week).\n23: * **Cyclicality ($C_t$)**: Non-periodic, longer-term fluctuations driven by macro-factors without a fixed structural length.\n24: \n<truncated 2503 bytes>\nion (PACF)**:\n67:   Measures direct correlation between $y_t$ and $y_{t-k}$ after partialing out linear correlations of intermediate lags $(y_{t-1}, \\dots, y_{t-k+1})$.\n68: \n69: | Model Specification | ACF Profile | PACF Profile |\n70: | :--- | :--- | :--- |\n71: | **AR($p$)** | Exponential decay or sinusoidal damping | Cuts off abruptly after lag $p$ |\n72: | **MA($q$)** | Cuts off abruptly after lag $q$ | Exponential decay or sinusoidal damping |\n73: | **ARMA($p, q$)** | Exponential decay after lag $q$ | Exponential decay after lag $p$ |\n74: \n75: ---\n76: \n77: ## 4. Parametric Time Series Models\n78: \n79: ### Autoregressive Model: AR($p$)\n80: Expresses $y_t$ as a linear combination of its $p$ past values:\n81: $$y_t = c + \\sum_{i=1}^p \\phi_i y_{t-i} + \\epsilon_t$$\n82: \n83: ### Moving Average Model: MA($q$)\n84: Expresses $y_t$ as a linear combination of $q$ past white noise residual shocks:\n85: $$y_t = \\mu + \\epsilon_t + \\sum_{j=1}^q \\theta_j \\epsilon_{t-j}$$\n86: \n87: ### Autoregressive Integrated Moving Average: ARIMA($p, d, q$)\n88: Combines AR and MA modeling after applying $d$-th order differencing to achieve stationarity:\n89: $$\\left(1 - \\sum_{i=1}^p \\phi_i L^i\\right) (1 - L)^d y_t = c + \\left(1 + \\sum_{j=1}^q \\theta_j L^j\\right) \\epsilon_t$$\n90: where $L$ is the backshift operator ($L^k y_t = y_{t-k}$).\n91: \n92: ### Seasonal ARIMA: SARIMA($p, d, q$) $\\times$ ($P, D, Q$)$_s$\n93: Extends ARIMA by adding seasonal autoregressive ($P$), differencing ($D$), and moving average ($Q$) terms with seasonal period length $s$.\n94: \n95: ### Multivariate Frameworks\n96: * **VAR (Vector Autoregression)**: Models dynamic interactions between vector components $\\mathbf{Y}_t = [y_{1,t}, y_{2,t}, \\dots, y_{m,t}]^T$.\n97: * **ARIMAX / Dynamic Regression**: Adds external covariates $X_t$ directly to the ARIMA mathematical formulation.\n98: \n99: ---\n100: \nThe above content does NOT show the entire file contents. If you need to view any lines of the file which were not shown to complete your task, call this tool again to view those lines.\n","truncated_fields":["content"]}

{"step_index":62,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:05:16Z","input_tokens":2828,"cache_read_tokens":43392,"output_tokens":1617,"tool_calls":[{"name":"write_to_file","args":{"CodeContent":"\"import pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Any\\nfrom lifelines import KaplanMeierFitter\\n\\n\\nclass EmpiricalConditionalBaseline:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Non-parametric conditional empirical baseline (PLAN.md Section 5.1).\\n    Given region R and elapsed duration t:\\n      - Filters historical alerts where duration D > t.\\n      - Remaining time distribution: Y_rem = D - t.\\n      - Produces P10, P50 (median), P90 and P(ends within 15/30/60 min).\\n      - Gracefully falls back to global (national) empirical distribution if region sample is small.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, min_samples: int = 10):\\n        self.min_samples = min_samples\\n        self.region_durations: Dict[str, np.ndarray] = {}\\n        self.global_durations: np.ndarray = np.array([])\\n\\n    def fit(self, df_alerts: pd.DataFrame, duration_col: str = \\\"duration_min\\\") -> \\\"EmpiricalConditionalBaseline\\\":\\n        self.global_durations = df_alerts[duration_col].dropna().values\\n        self.region_durations = {}\\n        for region_id, group in df_alerts.groupby(\\\"region_id\\\"):\\n            self.region_durations[region_id] = group[duration_col].dropna().values\\n        return self\\n\\n    def predict_remaining(\\n        self,\\n        region_id: str,\\n        elapsed_min: float,\\n        horizons_min: Optional[List[int]] = None,\\n    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:\\n        if horizons_min is None:\\n            horizons_min = [15, 30, 60]\\n\\n        durations = self.region_durations.get(region_id, self.global_durations)\\n        # Filter alerts that lasted longer than elapsed_min\\n        surviving = durations[durations > elapsed_min]\\n\\n        if len(surviving) < self.min_samples:\\n            # Fallback to national\\n            surviving = self.global_durations[self.global_durations > elapsed_min]\\n\\n        if len(surviving) == 0:\\n            # If elapsed_min exceeds all historical alerts, return default conservative estimate\\n            return {\\n                \\\"p10\\\": 5.0,\\n                \\\"p50\\\": 15.\n<truncated 2990 bytes>","Description":"\"Create baseline models module with Empirical Conditional Quantile and Kaplan-Meier estimators\"","Overwrite":"true","TargetFile":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/models/baseline.py\"","toolAction":"\"Writing baseline models module\"","toolSummary":"\"Create baseline models module\""}}],"truncated_fields":["tool_calls"]}

y_pred\\n    return float(np.mean(np.maximum(alpha * err, (alpha - 1.0) * err)))\\n\\n\\ndef interval_coverage(y_true: np.ndarray, lower: np.ndarray, upper: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Calculates empirical coverage: fraction of true values falling between lower and upper bounds.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    covered = (y_true >= lower) & (y_true <= upper)\\n    return float(np.mean(covered))\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_point_predictions(y_true: np.ndarray, y_pred: np.ndarray) -> Dict[str, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"MAE and Median Absolute Error in minutes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    abs_err = np.abs(y_true - y_pred)\\n    return {\\n        \\\"mae_min\\\": float(np.mean(abs_err)),\\n        \\\"median_ae_min\\\": float(np.median(abs_err)),\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_quantile_predictions(\\n    y_true: np.ndarray,\\n    p10: np.ndarray,\\n    p50: np.ndarray,\\n    p90: np.ndarray,\\n) -> Dict[str, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Evaluates median MAE, pinball losses, and P10-P90 interval coverage.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pt_metrics = evaluate_point_predictions(y_true, p50)\\n    loss_p10 = pinball_loss(y_true, p10, 0.10)\\n    loss_p50 = pinball_loss(y_true, p50, 0.50)\\n    loss_p90 = pinball_loss(y_true, p90, 0.90)\\n    cov_80 = interval_coverage(y_true, p10, p90)\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"mae_p50_min\\\": pt_metrics[\\\"mae_min\\\"],\\n        \\\"median_ae_p50_min\\\": pt_metrics[\\\"median_ae_min\\\"],\\n        \\\"pinball_loss_p10\\\": loss_p10,\\n        \\\"pinball_loss_p50\\\": loss_p50,\\n        \\\"pinball_loss_p90\\\": loss_p90,\\n        \\\"mean_pinball_loss\\\": float(np.mean([loss_p10, loss_p50, loss_p90])),\\n        \\\"coverage_p10_p90\\\": cov_80,\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_classification_probabilities(\\n    y_true_binary: np.ndarray,\\n    y_prob: np.ndarray,\\n) -> Dict[str, float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Evaluates probabi\n<truncated 526 bytes>","Description":"\"Create evaluation metrics module with pinball loss, coverage, MAE and Brier score\"","Overwrite":"true","TargetFile":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/evaluation/metrics.py\"","toolAction":"\"Writing evaluation metrics module\"","toolSummary":"\"Create evaluation metrics module\""}}],"truncated_fields":["tool_calls"]}

{"step_index":139,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:10:50Z","input_tokens":2308,"cache_read_tokens":77935,"output_tokens":747,"tool_calls":[{"name":"replace_file_content","args":{"AllowMultiple":"false","Description":"\"Optimize predict_batch in EmpiricalConditionalBaseline with memoization/dictionary lookup\"","EndLine":"95","Instruction":"\"Update predict_batch with memoization\"","ReplacementContent":"\"    def predict_batch(\\n        self,\\n        df_samples: pd.DataFrame,\\n        elapsed_col: str = \\\"elapsed_min\\\",\\n    ) -> pd.DataFrame:\\n        # Cache unique (region_id, elapsed) combinations for instantaneous vector lookup\\n        cache = {}\\n        unique_pairs = df_samples[[\\\"region_id\\\", elapsed_col]].drop_duplicates().values\\n        for reg, t in unique_pairs:\\n            res = self.predict_remaining(reg, t)\\n            cache[(reg, t)] = (res[\\\"p10\\\"], res[\\\"p50\\\"], res[\\\"p90\\\"])\\n\\n        p10s, p50s, p90s = [], [], []\\n        for reg, t in zip(df_samples[\\\"region_id\\\"].values, df_samples[elapsed_col].values):\\n            p10, p50, p90 = cache.get((reg, t), (5.0, 15.0, 30.0))\\n            p10s.append(p10)\\n            p50s.append(p50)\\n            p90s.append(p90)\\n\\n        preds = pd.DataFrame(index=df_samples.index)\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p10\\\"] = p10s\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p50\\\"] = p50s\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p90\\\"] = p90s\\n        return preds\"","StartLine":"72","TargetContent":"\"    def predict_batch(\\n        self,\\n        df_samples: pd.DataFrame,\\n        elapsed_col: str = \\\"elapsed_min\\\",\\n    ) -> pd.DataFrame:\\n        p10s, p50s, p90s = [], [], []\\n        for _, row in df_samples.iterrows():\\n            reg = row[\\\"region_id\\\"]\\n            t = row[elapsed_col]\\n            res = self.predict_remaining(reg, t)\\n            p10s.append(res[\\\"p10\\\"])\\n            p50s.append(res[\\\"p50\\\"])\\n            p90s.append(res[\\\"p90\\\"])\\n\\n        preds = pd.DataFrame(index=df_samples.index)\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p10\\\"] = p10s\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p50\\\"] = p50s\\n        preds[\\\"baseline_p90\\\"] = p90s\\n        return preds\"","TargetFile":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/models/baseline.py\"","toolAction":"\"Optimizing predict_batch\"","toolSummary":"\"Optimize predict_batch in baseline\""}}]}

Aligned with [PLAN.md](PLAN.md).\\n\\n> **Important Disclaimer:** This project is a statistical experiment and research benchmark. It is **not** a civil defense tool and must **never** be used in place of official alerts from the State Emergency Service of Ukraine or official air raid siren channels.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. Problem Formulation\\n\\nStandard regression directly on total duration is fundamentally flawed because real users query the system *while an alert is already in progress* (or while waiting in a quiet period). This is a conditional **time-to-event / survival analysis problem**:\\n\\n| Task | Event | Observation Clock | Censoring & Conditional Formulation |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| **Task A: Remaining Alert Duration** | Alert ends | Siren starts ($t=0$) | At elapsed time $t$, predict $P(\\\\text{ends in } \\\\Delta \\\\mid \\\\text{duration} > t)$ |\\n| **Task B: Time to Next Alert** | Next alert starts | Previous alert ends ($t_{quiet}=0$) | At quiet elapsed time $t_{quiet}$, predict $P(\\\\text{starts in } \\\\Delta \\\\mid \\\\text{gap} > t_{quiet})$ |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Methodology & Ladder of Models\\n\\nFollowing **PLAN.md Section 5**, models are evaluated against strong non-parametric baselines:\\n\\n1. **Empirical Conditional Quantile Baseline:** Non-parametric empirical conditional distribution derived from historical alerts that exceeded elapsed time $t$.\\n2. **Kaplan-Meier Survival Baseline:** Estimation of $S(t) = P(D > t)$ with conditional survival probability $S(\\\\Delta \\\\mid t) = \\\\frac{S(t + \\\\Delta)}{S(t)}$.\\n3. **LightGBM Quantile Regressors:** Gradient-boosted quantile regressions at $\\\\alpha \\\\in \\\\{0.10, 0.50, 0.90\\\\}$ to predict median remaining time and calibrated uncertainty intervals $[P10, P90]$.\\n4. **LightGBM Binary Probability Classifiers:** Predicting exact calibrated probabilities:\\n   - Task A: $P(\\\n<truncated 4157 bytes>","Description":"\"Update README.md with comprehensive project documentation, architecture, and instructions\"","Overwrite":"true","TargetFile":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/README.md\"","toolAction":"\"Writing README.md\"","toolSummary":"\"Update README.md\""}}],"truncated_fields":["tool_calls"]}

{"step_index":220,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:15:10Z","input_tokens":2331,"cache_read_tokens":112499,"output_tokens":1564,"content":"I have completed the setup, downloaded the historical dataset, implemented the model training skeleton code, and created the interactive exploratory notebook aligned with [PLAN.md](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/PLAN.md).\n\n---\n\n### What Was Built\n\n#### 1. Data Ingestion & Cleaning\n- **Raw Data Ingestion**: Downloaded the official Ukrainian air raid dataset (`299,275` records from March 2022 to September 2026) to [data/raw/official_data_uk.csv](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/data/raw/official_data_uk.csv).\n- **Episode Filtering & Normalization** ([src/alertpred/data/cleaning.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/data/cleaning.py)):\n  - Filtered to macroscopic oblast-level episodes (`65,172` cleaned episodes across 24 oblasts).\n  - Converted timestamps to UTC and eliminated sub-minute glitch alerts ($< 1$ min).\n  - Built inter-alert quiet episodes for **Task B** (`65,144` inter-alert quiet periods).\n  - Implemented strict chronological train/test splitting (80% train / 20% test on recent months) to prevent future data leakage.\n\n#### 2. Model Training Skeleton & Pipeline\n- **Conditional As-Of Sampling** ([src/alertpred/features/builder.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/features/builder.py)):\n  - Samples $t \\in [0, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 45, 60, 90, 120]$ min inside alerts to learn the conditional remaining time problem the app faces ($P(\\text{ends in } \\Delta \\mid \\text{duration} > t)$).\n  - Extracts cyclical hour and day-of-week sine/cosine features, regional history, and quiet elapsed times.\n- **Ladder of Models** ([src/alertpred/models/](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/models)):\n  - **Empirical Conditional Quantile Baseline**: Non-parametric conditional quantiles and probabilities.\n  - **Kaplan-Meier Baseline**: Survival curves $S(t) = P(D > t)$ via `lifelines`.\n  - **LightGBM Quantile Regressors**: Gradient-boosted quantile models at $\\alpha \\in \\{0.10, 0.50, 0.90\\}$ providing median predictions and $[P10, P90]$ uncertainty intervals.\n  - **Li\n<truncated 809 bytes>\nactive Jupyter Notebook\n- Created [notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb) covering:\n  1. Setup and imports\n  2. Data ingestion and cleaning checklist\n  3. Exploratory Data Analysis (duration distributions, diurnal patterns, quiet gaps)\n  4. Chronological train/test split\n  5. Empirical and Kaplan-Meier baselines\n  6. Feature engineering and \"as-of\" moment sampling\n  7. LightGBM quantile regression & probability modeling\n  8. Rigorous evaluation against baselines\n  9. Interactive scenario explorer for Ukrainian regions (Kyiv, Kharkiv, Lviv, Odesa, etc.)\n  10. Model serialization and reloading\n\n---\n\n### Test-Split Results & Real-Time Inference Demo\n\nThe pipeline was executed and validated on the chronological test split (episodes after January 2025):\n\n| Task | Median MAE | Median AE | $[P10, P90]$ Coverage | Mean Pinball Loss |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Empirical Baseline** | 103.00 min | 34.86 min | 64.6% | 36.64 |\n| **LightGBM Quantile** | **100.71 min** | 36.78 min | 61.4% | **36.01** |\n\n#### Sample Prediction Queries:\n\n- **Scenario 1: Active Alert in Kyiv (running for 30 minutes)**\n  - Predicted Remaining Duration (Median $P50$): **41.6 minutes**\n  - Predicted Uncertainty Range $[P10, P90]$: **$[2.9, 80.8]$ minutes**\n  - Probability alert ends within 15 min: **26.2%**\n  - Probability alert ends within 30 min: **39.6%**\n  - Probability alert ends within 60 min: **65.2%**\n\n- **Scenario 2: Quiet Period in Lviv (quiet for 3 hours)**\n  - Predicted Gap to Next Alert (Median $P50$): **2,494 minutes** (~41.5 hours)\n  - Probability alert starts within 1 hour: **3.0%**\n  - Probability alert starts within 3 hours: **6.7%**\n  - Probability alert starts within 6 hours: **10.7%**\n\n---\n\n### How to Run\n\n```bash\n# Activate the environment\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n\n# 1. Run the standalone training pipeline\nPYTHONPATH=src python -m alertpred.train\n\n# 2. Launch the Jupyter Notebook\njupyter notebook notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb\n```","truncated_fields":["content"]}

{"step_index":222,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:23:26Z","input_tokens":116712,"cache_read_tokens":0,"output_tokens":700,"content":"**Yes, fundamentally they run the same model training and evaluation logic**, but they are designed for two different stages of the workflow:\n\n| Aspect | [01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb) | [train.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/train.py) |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Primary Purpose** | **Exploration & Visual Analysis** | **Automation & Production Runs** |\n| **Interface** | Interactive Jupyter Notebook (cell by cell) | Non-interactive command-line script (CLI) |\n| **Visuals & EDA** | Includes plots (histograms, survival curves, boxplots, multi-region scenario charts) | Clean terminal logs with formatted metrics tables |\n| **Execution** | Manual in browser / VS Code / PyCharm | Single command: `python -m alertpred.train` |\n| **CLI Arguments** | Hardcoded or notebook variables | Supports flags: `--data`, `--output`, `--train-ratio` |\n| **Typical Use Case** | Experimenting with new features, debugging predictions, and visual validation | Automated re-training, scheduled cron jobs, CI/CD, or deployment |\n\nBoth import the exact same modules from [`src/alertpred`](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/) and produce the same serialized model artifact at `models/alertpred_pipeline.joblib`.\n\n### Recommended Workflow\n1. Use the **notebook** right now to inspect data distributions, evaluate curves, and test scenario predictions interactively.\n2. When you are ready to integrate with the main app (e.g. Streamlit or scheduled updates), use **`train.py`** to train and refresh the saved models with a single headless command.","thinking":"Both files implement the same model training logic end-to-end. However, they are designed for different stages.\n\nThe first, an interactive notebook, facilitates exploration, visual validation, and experimentation, allowing for model development and debugging. The second, a standalone Python script, is built for automated training and deployment in a production environment.\n\n\n"}

{"step_index":230,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:33:32Z","input_tokens":3091,"cache_read_tokens":120541,"output_tokens":908,"thinking":"Calculating neighbor alert overlap requires determining if an alert interval in a neighbor region overlaps with the active interval in the original region. The overlap condition is defined as: the start of the neighbor's alert interval must be less than the end of the original interval, and the end of the neighbor's interval must be greater than the start of the original interval. This condition needs verification.\n\n\n","tool_calls":[{"name":"run_command","args":{"CommandLine":"\"PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python3 -c \\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom alertpred.config import OBLAST_NEIGHBORS\\nfrom alertpred.data.cleaning import load_raw_data, clean_alerts\\n\\ndf = clean_alerts(load_raw_data())\\n\\n# Hourly grid\\nstart_min = df['started_at'].min().floor('h')\\nend_max = df['finished_at'].max().ceil('h')\\nhourly_idx = pd.date_range(start_min, end_max, freq='1h', tz='UTC')\\nprint('Hourly grid points:', len(hourly_idx))\\n\\n# Build binary matrix (hours x regions)\\n# For fast lookup, map intervals\\nreg_cols = sorted(df['region_id'].unique())\\nmatrix = np.zeros((len(hourly_idx), len(reg_cols)), dtype=bool)\\nreg_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(reg_cols)}\\n\\nfor _, row in df.iterrows():\\n    c_idx = reg_to_idx[row['region_id']]\\n    s_idx = max(0, int((row['started_at'] - start_min).total_seconds() // 3600))\\n    e_idx = min(len(hourly_idx), int((row['finished_at'] - start_min).total_seconds() // 3600) + 1)\\n    matrix[s_idx:e_idx, c_idx] = True\\n\\nprint('Matrix shape:', matrix.shape)\\n# Simultaneous overlap: dot product\\noverlap_hours = (matrix.T.astype(int) @ matrix.astype(int))\\nprint('Simultaneous overlap computed in ms! Kyiv City & Kyiv Oblast co-alert hours:',\\n      overlap_hours[reg_to_idx['kyiv_city'], reg_to_idx['kyiv']])\\n\\\"\"","Cwd":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk\"","WaitMsBeforeAsync":"10000","toolAction":"\"Testing hourly matrix co-occurrence calculation\"","toolSummary":"\"Test co-occurrence calculation\""}}]}

{"step_index":234,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:33:53Z","input_tokens":2667,"cache_read_tokens":122660,"output_tokens":1050,"thinking":"The complete simultaneous hourly matrix for all 24 oblasts over 4.5 years has been calculated rapidly. This matrix will facilitate insightful metrics, starting with Jaccard Similarity (Co-Occurrence Index) to quantify synchronization between any two oblasts, computed using the intersection and union of their respective hourly data. The next metric will be conditional co-occurrence.\n\n\n","tool_calls":[{"name":"run_command","args":{"CommandLine":"\"PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python3 -c \\\"\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom alertpred.config import OBLAST_NEIGHBORS\\nfrom alertpred.data.cleaning import load_raw_data, clean_alerts\\n\\ndf = clean_alerts(load_raw_data())\\nstart_min = df['started_at'].min().floor('h')\\nend_max = df['finished_at'].max().ceil('h')\\nhourly_idx = pd.date_range(start_min, end_max, freq='1h', tz='UTC')\\n\\nreg_cols = sorted(df['region_id'].unique())\\nmatrix = np.zeros((len(hourly_idx), len(reg_cols)), dtype=bool)\\nreg_to_idx = {r: i for i, r in enumerate(reg_cols)}\\n\\nfor _, row in df.iterrows():\\n    c_idx = reg_to_idx[row['region_id']]\\n    s_idx = max(0, int((row['started_at'] - start_min).total_seconds() // 3600))\\n    e_idx = min(len(hourly_idx), int((row['finished_at'] - start_min).total_seconds() // 3600) + 1)\\n    matrix[s_idx:e_idx, c_idx] = True\\n\\n# Neighbor simultaneous rate\\nresults = []\\nfor reg in reg_cols:\\n    c_idx = reg_to_idx[reg]\\n    reg_hours = matrix[:, c_idx]\\n    total_active_hours = reg_hours.sum()\\n    \\n    neighbors = [n for n in OBLAST_NEIGHBORS.get(reg, []) if n in reg_to_idx]\\n    if not neighbors or total_active_hours == 0:\\n        continue\\n    \\n    neighbor_indices = [reg_to_idx[n] for n in neighbors]\\n    # At least one neighbor active\\n    any_neighbor_active = matrix[:, neighbor_indices].any(axis=1)\\n    co_active_hours = (reg_hours & any_neighbor_active).sum()\\n    pct_co_active = co_active_hours / total_active_hours\\n    \\n    results.append({\\n        'region_id': reg,\\n        'total_active_hours': total_active_hours,\\n        'co_active_hours': co_active_hours,\\n        'pct_neighbor_simultaneous': pct_co_active,\\n        'neighbor_count': len(neighbors)\\n    })\\n\\nres_df = pd.DataFrame(results).sort_values('pct_neighbor_simultaneous', ascending=False)\\nprint('Neighbor Co-Occurrence Sample (Top 5):')\\nprint(res_df.head(5))\\nprint('Neighbor Co-Occurrence Sample (Bottom 5):')\\nprint(res_df.tail(5))\\n\\\"\"","Cwd":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI\"","WaitMsBeforeAsync":"10000","toolAction":"\"Testing neighbor co-occurrence computation\"","toolSummary":"\"Test neighbor co-occurrence\""}}]}

Alert Duration: Mean vs. Median & Extreme Skewness (Section 3.2)\nComparing the mean and median reveals heavy right-tail skewness caused by long multi-hour drone and missile attacks:\n\n| Oblast | Mean Duration | Median Duration ($P50$) | 10th Percentile ($P10$) | 90th Percentile ($P90$) | Std Dev |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Sumy Oblast** | **117.7 min** | 52.2 min | 19.5 min | **275.9 min** (4.6 h) | 159.5 min |\n| **Donetsk Oblast** | **107.7 min** | 60.3 min | 21.0 min | **235.3 min** (3.9 h) | 133.4 min |\n| **Chernihiv Oblast*\n<truncated 1141 bytes>\nal Average Co-Occurrence**: When an oblast is under alert, in **94.7% of all active hours at least one of its direct neighboring oblasts is also under alert**.\n- **Central & Western Regions (>99% Simultaneous)**:\n  - Chernivtsi: **99.8%**\n  - Poltava: **99.7%**\n  - Ivano-Frankivsk: **99.6%**\n  - Zakarpattia: **99.5%**\n  - Rivne: **99.3%**\n  *Alerts in these regions almost never happen in isolation; they are virtually always part of a wider multi-region attack wave.*\n- **Border / Frontline Regions**:\n  - Kharkiv: **90.6%**\n  - Sumy: **87.5%**\n  - Donetsk: **81.1%**\n  *Frontline regions experience occasional localized artillery or tactical air alerts, but still share >80% of their alert time with neighboring regions.*\n- **Pairwise Correlation Heatmap**:\n  The correlation heatmap reveals distinct regional clusters:\n  1. *East cluster:* Kharkiv $\\leftrightarrow$ Donetsk $\\leftrightarrow$ Zaporizhzhia $\\leftrightarrow$ Dnipropetrovsk.\n  2. *Central / Transit cluster:* Sumy $\\leftrightarrow$ Poltava $\\leftrightarrow$ Cherkasy $\\leftrightarrow$ Kyiv.\n  3. *West cluster:* Lviv $\\leftrightarrow$ Ivano-Frankivsk $\\leftrightarrow$ Ternopil $\\leftrightarrow$ Volyn $\\leftrightarrow$ Rivne.\n\n---\n\n### What's in the Updated Notebook\nIn [notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb), you can execute Section 3 to see:\n1. **Ranked Horizontal Bar Chart** of total alert counts per oblast.\n2. **Monthly National Timeline** showing attack volume trends from 2022 to 2026.\n3. **Side-by-Side Horizontal Bar Chart**: Mean vs. Median duration for all oblasts.\n4. **Diurnal Attack Chart**: Alert starts by local hour (Europe/Kyiv time) showing night attack peaks.\n5. **Neighbor Co-Occurrence Ranking Chart**: Percentage of active hours with concurrent neighbor alerts.\n6. **Full $24 \\times 24$ Heatmap**: Pairwise simultaneous alert correlation across all Ukrainian oblasts.\n7. **Inter-Alert Quiet Gap Distribution**: Median quiet hours between consecutive alerts (Task B).","thinking":"The frequency of alerts varies significantly across oblasts, with frontline regions experiencing a drastically higher rate than western regions; In Python, re-running `from alertpred.config import OBLAST_NEIGHBORS` does not re-read modified files from disk by default — it queries the existing in-memory module, triggering `ImportError`.\n\n---\n\n### How to Fix\n\nYou have two simple options:\n\n#### Option 1: Restart the Jupyter Kernel (Recommended)\nIn your Jupyter / IDE interface:\n- Click **Kernel $\\rightarrow$ Restart Kernel** (or click the restart icon 🔄), then re-run the cells.\n\n#### Option 2: Automatic Reloading (Already Added to the Notebook)\nI have updated cell 1 of [01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb) to include IPython's `autoreload` extension:\n```python\n%load_ext autoreload\n%autoreload 2\n```\nOnce you restart the kernel and run this cell once, Jupyter will automatically detect any future edits to `.py` files in `src/` without needing kernel restarts."}

{"step_index":292,"source":"MODEL","type":"GENERIC","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:48:54Z","content":"Created At: 2026-10-10T12:48:54+03:00\nCompleted At: 2026-10-10T12:48:57+03:00\nThe search for \"\"ukraine\" oblasts geojson raw githubusercontent\" returned the following summary:\nFor access to Ukraine oblast-level GeoJSON files from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, you can use the following established resources:\n\n### geoBoundaries (Highly Recommended)\nThis is a standard, open-source repository for administrative boundaries worldwide[1].\n*   **ADM1 (Oblast level) GeoJSON:** [https://github.com/wmgeolab/geoBoundaries/raw/9469f09/releaseData/gbOpen/UKR/ADM1/geoBoundaries-UKR-ADM1.geojson](https://github.com/wmgeolab/geoBoundaries/raw/9469f09/releaseData/gbOpen/UKR/ADM1/geoBoundaries-UKR-ADM1.geojson)[2]\n*   **Simplified version (smaller file size):** [https://github.com/wmgeolab/geoBoundaries/raw/9469f09/releaseData/gbOpen/UKR/ADM1/geoBoundaries-UKR-ADM1_simplified.geojson](https://github.com/wmgeolab/geoBoundaries/raw/9469f09/releaseData/gbOpen/UKR/ADM1/geoBoundaries-UKR-ADM1_simplified.geojson)[1]\n\n### Other Community Repositories\n*   **EugeneBorshch/ukraine_geojson:** A repository dedicated to Ukraine and its regions in GeoJSON format[3][4]. You can navigate to their GitHub repository to find the raw links for specific files[3].\n*   **Humanitarian Data Exchange (HDX):** Provides official and validated administrative boundary datasets[5]. While they often link to ZIP files, their platform is the primary source for humanitarian-grade GeoJSON in Ukraine[1][5].\n\n**Tip:** When using GitHub raw links, ensure you are using the URL that starts with `raw.githubusercontent.com`. {"step_index":311,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:50:18Z","input_tokens":3146,"cache_read_tokens":172018,"output_tokens":1332,"thinking":"The region panel's rendering requirements involve a status badge displaying \"🚨 ALERT\" with remaining time when active or \"✅ Quiet\" with elapsed time when inactive. Active alerts require displaying a predicted end time, calculated as the median remaining time plus the estimated completion clock time in Kyiv time, along with an indication of uncertainty.\n\n\n","tool_calls":[{"name":"write_to_file","args":{"CodeContent":"\"from typing import Dict, Any, List\\nimport streamlit as st\\nimport plotly.graph_objects as go\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport datetime\\n\\n\\ndef render_probability_bars(probabilities: Dict[str, float], is_active: bool):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Renders calibrated probability progress indicators.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    st.markdown(\\\"#### Forecast Probabilities\\\")\\n    \\n    if is_active:\\n        items = [\\n            (\\\"Ends within 15 minutes\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_ends_within_15m\\\", 0.0)),\\n            (\\\"Ends within 30 minutes\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_ends_within_30m\\\", 0.0)),\\n            (\\\"Ends within 60 minutes\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_ends_within_60m\\\", 0.0)),\\n        ]\\n    else:\\n        items = [\\n            (\\\"Alert starts within 1 hour\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_starts_within_1h\\\", 0.0)),\\n            (\\\"Alert starts within 3 hours\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_starts_within_3h\\\", 0.0)),\\n            (\\\"Alert starts within 6 hours\\\", probabilities.get(\\\"prob_starts_within_6h\\\", 0.0)),\\n        ]\\n\\n    for label, prob in items:\\n        pct = int(prob * 100)\\n        col1, col2 = st.columns([3, 1])\\n        with col1:\\n            st.write(f\\\"**{label}**\\\")\\n            st.progress(prob)\\n        with col2:\\n            st.markdown(f\\\"<h3 style='margin:0; text-align:right;'>{pct}%</h3>\\\", unsafe_allow_html=True)\\n\\n\\ndef create_remaining_time_curve(elapsed_min: float, median_remaining: float, p10: float, p90: float) -> go.Figure:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Generates an illustrative conditional remaining-time distribution curve.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Approximate smooth lognormal distribution matching P10, P50, P90\\n    x = np.linspace(0, max(120, p90 * 1.5), 100)\\n    # Using simple smoothed curve peaked at median\\n    sigma = (p90 - p10) / 2.56 if (p90 - p10) > 0 else 15.0\\n    y = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - median_remaining) / (sigma + 1e-5)) ** 2)\\n    y = y / y.max()\\n\\n    fig = go.Figure()\\n    fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(\\n        x=x,\\n        y=y,\\n        mode=\\\"lines\\\",\\n        line=dict(color=\\\"#38bdf8\\\", width=3),\\n        fill=\\\"tozeroy\\\"\n<truncated 853 bytes>","Description":"\"Create UI components for probability bars and conditional distribution curves\"","Overwrite":"true","TargetFile":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/app/components.py\"","toolAction":"\"Writing UI components module\"","toolSummary":"\"Create UI components module\""}}],"truncated_fields":["tool_calls"]}

Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.\n80: ## 5. Quickstart\n81: \n82: ### Environment Setup\n83: \n84: ```bash\n85: # Create virtual environment\n86: python3 -m venv .venv\n87: source .venv/bin/activate\n88: \n89: # Install dependencies\n90: pip install -r requirements.txt\n91: ```\n92: \n93: ### Running the Training Pipeline\n94: \n95: To train the models, run evaluation on the test set, and save model artifacts:\n96: \n97: ```bash\n98: PYTHONPATH=src python -m alertpred.train --data data/raw/official_data_uk.csv\n99: ```\n100: \n101: ### Interactive Jupyter Notebook\n102: \n103: Open and explore the step-by-step pipeline:\n104: \n105: ```bash\n106: jupyter notebook notebooks/01_model_training_pipeline.ipynb\n107: ```\n108: \n109: ---\n110: \n111: ## 6. Sample Live Inference\n112: \n113: ```python\n114: from alertpred.models.quantile_lgbm import AlertPredictionPipeline\n115: \n116: # Load serialized pipeline\n117: pipeline = AlertPredictionPipeline.load(\"models/alertpred_pipeline.joblib\")\n118: \n119: # Scenario 1: Active alert in Kyiv that has lasted 30 minutes\n120: res_active = pipeline.predict_active_alert(region_id=\"kyiv_city\", elapsed_min=30.0)\n121: print(res_active)\n122: # Output:\n123: # Predicted remaining median: 41.6 min\n124: # Uncertainty range [P10, P90]: [2.9, 80.8] min\n125: # Probabilities:\n126: #   Ends within 15 min: 26.2%\n127: #   Ends within 30 min: 39.6%\n128: #   Ends within 60 min: 65.2%\n129: \n130: # Scenario 2: Quiet region in Lviv quiet for 3 hours\n131: res_quiet = pipeline.predict_quiet_region(region_id=\"lviv\", elapsed_quiet_min=180.0)\n132: print(res_quiet)\n133: # Output:\n134: # Predicted next alert gap: 2494.3 min (~41.5 hrs)\n135: # Probabilities:\n136: #   Starts within 1h: 3.0%\n137: #   Starts within 3h: 6.7%\n138: #   Starts within 6h: 10.7%\n139: ```\nThe above content does NOT show the entire file contents. If you need to view any lines of the file which were not shown to complete your task, call this tool again to view those lines.\n"}

{"step_index":329,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-10T09:51:43Z","input_tokens":2359,"cache_read_tokens":181412,"output_tokens":1027,"content":"The minimalistic Web App MVP is implemented and running, matching the specifications in [PLAN.md](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/PLAN.md) (Section 1, Section 8, and Section 10 Block 5).\n\n---\n\n### What Was Built\n\n#### 1. Streamlit Application Core ([app.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/app.py))\n- **Dark Theme Styling**: Configured via [.streamlit/config.toml](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/.streamlit/config.toml) with Ukraine-themed dark palette (deep slate `#0e1117`, alert crimson `#ef4444`, and quiet dark slate `#1e293b`).\n- **National Status Header**: Displays national alert count (e.g. `5 / 25 Oblasts`), alert status indicator, current Europe/Kyiv clock time, and model engine badge.\n- **Side-by-Side Responsive Layout**:\n  - **Left (60%)**: Interactive National Siren Map.\n  - **Right (40%)**: Selected Region Forecast Panel.\n\n#### 2. Interactive Choropleth Map ([src/alertpred/app/map.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/app/map.py))\n- Downloaded and validated the official simplified administrative GeoJSON for Ukraine's 24 oblasts, Kyiv City, and Crimea ([data/geo/ukraine_oblasts.geojson](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/data/geo/ukraine_oblasts.geojson)).\n- Oblasts are dynamically colored by current status:\n  - 🚨 **Red (`#ef4444`)**: Active air raid alert.\n  - ⬛ **Slate (`#1e293b`)**: Quiet / safe.\n- Hover tooltips show region name, current status, and elapsed duration / quiet hours.\n\n#### 3. Real-Time Expiration & Gap Predictor ([src/alertpred/app/components.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/app/components.py))\nFor whichever region is selected:\n- **If Active Alert:**\n  - Status badge: `🚨 ACTIVE AIR RAID ALERT · Running for X minutes`\n  - **Predicted Remaining Duration (Median $P50$)** with estimated clock time of expiration in Kyiv time (e.g., `~42 min (est. end at 13:28)`).\n  - **Uncertainty Range $[P10, P90]$**: `Likely interval: 3 – 81 minutes`.\n  - **Probability Bars**:\n    * Ends within 15 min\n    * Ends within 30 min\n    * Ends within 60 min\n  - **Remaining Time Distribution Curve**: Illustrative conditional density showing the likelihood spread peaked at median with $P10$–$P90$ shaded.\n- **If No Active Alert (Quiet Period):**\n  - Status badge: `✅ NO ACTIVE ALERT · Quiet for Y hours`\n  - **Predicted Time to Next Siren (Median $P50$)** with uncertainty range.\n  - **Probability Bars**:\n    * Next siren within 1 hour\n    * Next siren within 3 hours\n    * Next siren within 6 hours\n\n#### 4. Live API & Simulation Controls ([src/alertpred/ingestion/live.py](file:///Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI/src/alertpred/ingestion/live.py))\n- Ready for live `alerts.in.ua` API integration (via `ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN`).\n- **Built-in Simulation Mode**: Works out of the box with realistic current snapshots.\n- **Interactive Sandbox in Sidebar**: Includes a `\"Simulate Custom State\"` toggle that lets you test any hypothetical scenario (e.g., test what the model predicts for Kyiv or Lviv if an alert has lasted 15, 45, or 90 minutes).\n\n#### 5. Safety Disclaimer\n- Prominent footer emphasizing that predictions are statistical research estimates and must never replace official sirens or Civil Defense instructions.\n\n---\n\n### How to Access the App\n\nThe app is running locally:\n- **URL**: [http://localhost:8501](http://localhost:8501)\n\nTo re-launch it at any time from the terminal:\n```bash\nPYTHONPATH=src streamlit run app.py\n```"}

A reversed winding order would cause the rendering library to interpret the polygon as encompassing the entire region outside of it.\n\n\n","tool_calls":[{"name":"run_command","args":{"CommandLine":"\"PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python3 -c \\\"\\nimport json\\nwith open('data/geo/ukraine_oblasts.geojson', 'r') as f:\\n    gj = json.load(f)\\n\\n# Calculate signed area of polygon rings\\ndef signed_area(ring):\\n    return sum((x2 - x1) * (y2 + y1) for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in zip(ring[:-1], ring[1:]))\\n\\nfor feat in gj['features']:\\n    name = feat['properties']['shapeName']\\n    iso = feat['properties']['shapeISO']\\n    geom = feat['geometry']\\n    rings = geom['coordinates'] if geom['type'] == 'Polygon' else [p[0] for p in geom['coordinates']]\\n    areas = [signed_area(r) for r in rings]\\n    # In standard planar cartesian, positive signed area with this formula means clockwise (or counterclockwise depending on convention)\\n    print(f'{name} ({iso}): ring count={len(rings)}, areas={[round(a, 2) for a in areas]}')\\n\\\"\"","Cwd":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI\"","WaitMsBeforeAsync":"5000","toolAction":"\"Checking GeoJSON polygon winding order\"","toolSummary":"\"Check polygon winding order\""}}]}

This suggests a possible coordinate order issue or a misinterpretation of ring direction.\n\n\n","tool_calls":[{"name":"run_command","args":{"CommandLine":"\"PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python3 -c \\\"\\nimport json\\n\\nwith open('data/geo/ukraine_oblasts.geojson', 'r') as f:\\n    gj = json.load(f)\\n\\ndef shoelace(ring):\\n    n = len(ring)\\n    return 0.5 * sum(ring[i][0] * ring[(i+1)%n][1] - ring[(i+1)%n][0] * ring[i][1] for i in range(n))\\n\\nfor feat in gj['features']:\\n    name = feat['properties']['shapeName']\\n    geom = feat['geometry']\\n    rings = geom['coordinates'] if geom['type'] == 'Polygon' else [p[0] for p in geom['coordinates']]\\n    areas = [shoelace(r) for r in rings]\\n    is_ccw = [a > 0 for a in areas]\\n    print(f'{name}: is_ccw={is_ccw}')\\n\\\"\"","Cwd":"\"/Users/allakovalcuk/Agentic_AI\"","WaitMsBeforeAsync":"5000","toolAction":"\"Checking Shoelace ring orientation\"","toolSummary":"\"Check Shoelace orientation\""}}]}

{"step_index":410,"source":"MODEL","type":"GENERIC","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-10-12T08:35:14Z","content":"Created At: 2026-10-12T11:35:14+03:00\nCompleted At: 2026-10-12T11:35:18+03:00\nThe search for \"use_container_width\" returned the following summary:\n`use_container_width` is a parameter in Streamlit used to control the width of various UI elements (such as `st.dataframe`, `st.image`, and `st.altair_chart`)[1][2][3].\n\n### Current Status: Deprecation\n**`use_container_width` is deprecated** and will be removed in a future version of Streamlit[4][5][6]. You should transition to using the `width` parameter instead[4][5]:\n\n*   **To replace `use_container_width=True`**: Use **`width=\"stretch\"`**[5][6]. This forces the element to expand to the full width of its parent container[5][3].\n*   **To replace `use_container_width=False`**: Use **`width=\"content\"`**[5][3]. This sizes the element according to its content (without exceeding the parent container's width)[5].\n\n### What it Does\nWhen set to `True`, the parameter forces the component to expand and fill the entire width of the column or container it resides in[7][2][3]. This is particularly useful for making tables, charts, and images more readable on wider screens or when using `st.set_page_config(layout=\"wide\")`[7][2].\n\n### Common Issues\n*   **Not Working:** If you find `use_container_width` is not working as expected, ensure your version of Streamlit is up to date[2].\n*   **Conflicts:** In some components (like `st.button`), the parameter may be ignored if other arguments (like `help`) are defined[8].\n*   **Availability:** While it has been widely used, newer components or specific configurations may favor the updated `width` API[3][9].\n\nSources:\n[1] buttons) so that they automatically span the entire width of their parent container[1][2][3].\n\n### Important Update: Deprecation Warning\nAs of recent updates, `use_container_width` is being **deprecated** and is scheduled to be removed after **December 31, 2025**[4][5]. \n\nYou should transition to using the `width` parameter instead[4][6]:\n*   **For `use_container_width=True`**: Use `width=\"stretch\"`[4][3][6].\n*   **For `use_container_width=False`**: Use `width=\"content\"`[6].\n\n### Summary of Use\n*   **Functionality**: When set to `True`, it forces the element (e.g., a `st.dataframe` or `st.plotly_chart`) to resize and fill the horizontal space available in the column or page layout[7][2][3].\n*   **Common Usage**: It is frequently used to make visualizations and tables more readable by utilizing all available screen real estate[7][2].\n*   **Troubleshooting**: If you encounter issues where elements do not resize as expected or you receive an unexpected keyword argument error, ensure your Streamlit version is up to date, as this feature was introduced in earlier versions and is now being phased out in favor of the more flexible `width`
